feats[:-1], dim=-1)  # (time-1, batch)\\n    delta_min = deltas.min(dim=0, keepdim=True).values\\n    delta_max = deltas.max(dim=0, keepdim=True).values\\n    delta_norm = (deltas - delta_min) / (delta_max - delta_min + 1e-8)\\n    preferred_dt = 1.0 + (eff - 1.0) * (1.0 - delta_norm)\\n    dt_indices = torch.arange(1, max_dt + 1, dtype=torch.float32, device=feats.device)\\n    sigma = 1.0\\n    log_probs = -0.5 * ((preferred_dt.unsqueeze(-1) - dt_indices) / sigma) ** 2\\n    if eff < max_dt:\\n        log_probs[..., eff:] = -1e9\\n    target_dist = F.softmax(log_probs, dim=-1)\\n    return target_dist\\n\\n\\ndef gate_loss_fn(gate_logits, target_dist):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Cross-entropy between gate predictions and supervision target.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    log_probs = F.log_softmax(gate_logits, dim=-1)\\n    return -(target_dist * log_probs).sum(dim=-1)\\n\\n\\nclass RewardEMA:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"running mean and std\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self, device, alpha=1e-2):\\n        self.device = device\\n        self.alpha = alpha\\n        self.range = torch.tensor([0.05, 0.95], device=device)\\n\\n    def __call__(self, x, ema_vals):\\n        flat_x = torch.flatten(x.detach())\\n        x_quantile = torch.quantile(input=flat_x, q=self.range)\\n        # this should be in-place operation\\n        ema_vals[:] = self.alpha * x_quantile + (1 - self.alpha) * ema_vals\\n        scale = torch.clip(ema_vals[1] - ema_vals[0], min=1.0)\\n        offset = ema_vals[0]\\n        return offset.detach(), scale.detach()\\n\\n\\nclass WorldModel(nn.Module):\\n    def __init__(self, obs_space, act_space, step, config):\\n        super(WorldModel, self).__init__()\\n        self._step = step\\n        self._use_amp = True if config.precision == 16 else False\\n        self._config = config\\n        shapes = {k: tuple(v.shape) for k, v in obs_space.spaces.items()}\\n        self.encoder = networks.MultiEncoder(shapes, **config.encoder)\\n        self.embed_size = self.encoder.outdim\\n        self.dynamics = networks.RSSM(\\n            config.dyn_stoch,\\n            config.dyn_deter,\\n            config.dyn_hidden,\\n            config.dyn_rec_depth,\\n            config.dyn_discrete,\\n            config.act,\\n            config.norm,\\n            config.dyn_mean_act,\\n            config.dyn_std_act,\\n            config.dyn_min_std,\\n            config.unimix_ratio,\\n            config.initial,\\n            config.num_actions,\\n            self.embed_size,\\n            config.device,\\n            max_dt=(2 if getattr(config, \\\"gate_type\\\", \\\"gumbel\\\") == \\\"bernoulli_st\\\" else config.gate_max_dt) if config.gate_enabled else 1,\\n            dt_embed_dim=config.gate_dt_embed_dim,\\n        )\\n        # plan_015B: freeze dt_emb to test if TRAINING (not existence) causes CIVO\\n        if getattr(config, 'dt_emb_freeze', False) and self.dynamics._dt_embedding is not None:\\n            self.dynamics._dt_embedding.weight.requires_grad_(False)\\n        self.heads = nn.ModuleDict()\\n        if config.dyn_discrete:\\n            feat_size = config.dyn_stoch * config.dyn_discrete + config.dyn_deter\\n        else:\\n            feat_size = config.dyn_stoch + config.dyn_deter\\n        self.heads[\\\"decoder\\\"] = net\\n\\n... _rg\\n                    )\\n\\n            metrics = self._model_opt(mean_model_loss, self.parameters())\\n\\n            if _sgs_handle is not None:\\n                _sgs_handle.remove()\\n\\n\\n        # Store scalar metrics to avoid keeping (batch,time) arrays until the next log step.\\n        metrics.update(\\n            {f\\\"{name}_loss\\\": to_np(torch.mean(loss)) for name, loss in losses.items()}\\n        )\\n        metrics[\\\"kl_free\\\"] = kl_free\\n        metrics[\\\"dyn_scale\\\"] = dyn_scale\\n        metrics[\\\"rep_scale\\\"] = rep_scale\\n        metrics[\\\"dyn_loss\\\"] = to_np(torch.mean(dyn_loss))\\n        metrics[\\\"rep_loss\\\"] = to_np(torch.mean(rep_loss))\\n        metrics[\\\"kl\\\"] = to_np(torch.mean(kl_value))\\n        with torch.cuda.amp.autocast(self._use_amp):\\n            metrics[\\\"prior_ent\\\"] = to_np(\\n                torch.mean(self.dynamics.get_dist(prior).entropy())\\n            )\\n            metrics[\\\"post_ent\\\"] = to_np(\\n                torch.mean(self.dynamics.get_dist(post).entropy())\\n            )\\n            context = dict(\\n                embed=embed,\\n                feat=self.dynamics.get_feat(post),\\n                kl=kl_value,\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}

````text
Let me trace through the actual optimizer flow more carefully to see how gradients are being managed between training steps. Let me look for the specific lines where actor and value losses are computed and optimized.

From what I read above, `RequiresGrad(self)` only manages the ImagBehavior parameters, not the world model's dt_embedding. The code disables gradients for dt_embedding.weight inside the context manager, but here's the problem — it never gets re-enabled after the block exits, so in the next training step when the world model trains, the dt_embedding won't receive any gradients even though it should. because `requires_grad` is False. The hook-based SGS in WorldModel._train() also tries to compute gradients on `dt_embedding.weight`, which would fail or return None since requires_grad is False.

There's a config flag `dt_emb_freeze` that freezes dt_embedding in WorldModel._train(), but for `full_sgs` mode, the real issue is in the ImagBehavior training flow: the `RequiresGrad` context manager enables gradients for ImagBehavior parameters during actor/value optimization, but it never re-enables `dt_embedding.weight` afterward. So after ImagBehavior training completes, dt_embedding stays frozen, and when WorldModel._train() runs in the next iteration, it can't compute the gradients it needs. The training sequence reveals the real problem: WorldModel trains dt_emb successfully in the first iteration, but then ImagBehavior immediately disables requires_grad and never restores it, so dt_emb stays frozen from iteration two onward. The hook-based SGS approach in WorldModel should handle everything, but ImagBehavior's requires_grad manipulation breaks the whole mechanism. feats[:-1], dim=-1)  # (time-1, batch)\\n    delta_min = deltas.min(dim=0, keepdim=True).values\\n    delta_max = deltas.max(dim=0, keepdim=True).values\\n    delta_norm = (deltas - delta_min) / (delta_max - delta_min + 1e-8)\\n    preferred_dt = 1.0 + (eff - 1.0) * (1.0 - delta_norm)\\n    dt_indices = torch.arange(1, max_dt + 1, dtype=torch.float32, device=feats.device)\\n    sigma = 1.0\\n    log_probs = -0.5 * ((preferred_dt.unsqueeze(-1) - dt_indices) / sigma) ** 2\\n    if eff < max_dt:\\n        log_probs[..., eff:] = -1e9\\n    target_dist = F.softmax(log_probs, dim=-1)\\n    return target_dist\\n\\n\\ndef gate_loss_fn(gate_logits, target_dist):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Cross-entropy between gate predictions and supervision target.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    log_probs = F.log_softmax(gate_logits, dim=-1)\\n    return -(target_dist * log_probs).sum(dim=-1)\\n\\n\\nclass RewardEMA:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"running mean and std\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self, device, alpha=1e-2):\\n        self.device = device\\n        self.alpha = alpha\\n        self.range = torch.tensor([0.05, 0.95], device=device)\\n\\n    def __call__(self, x, ema_vals):\\n        flat_x = torch.flatten(x.detach())\\n        x_quantile = torch.quantile(input=flat_x, q=self.range)\\n        # this should be in-place operation\\n        ema_vals[:] = self.alpha * x_quantile + (1 - self.alpha) * ema_vals\\n        scale = torch.clip(ema_vals[1] - ema_vals[0], min=1.0)\\n        offset = ema_vals[0]\\n        return offset.detach(), scale.detach()\\n\\n\\nclass WorldModel(nn.Module):\\n    def __init__(self, obs_space, act_space, step, config):\\n        super(WorldModel, self).__init__()\\n        self._step = step\\n        self._use_amp = True if config.precision == 16 else False\\n        self._config = config\\n        shapes = {k: tuple(v.shape) for k, v in obs_space.spaces.items()}\\n        self.encoder = networks.MultiEncoder(shapes, **config.encoder)\\n        self.embed_size = self.encoder.outdim\\n        self.dynamics = networks.RSSM(\\n            config.dyn_stoch,\\n            config.dyn_deter,\\n            config.dyn_hidden,\\n            config.dyn_rec_depth,\\n            config.dyn_discrete,\\n            config.act,\\n            config.norm,\\n            config.dyn_mean_act,\\n            config.dyn_std_act,\\n            config.dyn_min_std,\\n            config.unimix_ratio,\\n            config.initial,\\n            config.num_actions,\\n            self.embed_size,\\n            config.device,\\n            max_dt=(2 if getattr(config, \\\"gate_type\\\", \\\"gumbel\\\") == \\\"bernoulli_st\\\" else config.gate_max_dt) if config.gate_enabled else 1,\\n            dt_embed_dim=config.gate_dt_embed_dim,\\n        )\\n        # plan_015B: freeze dt_emb to test if TRAINING (not existence) causes CIVO\\n        if getattr(config, 'dt_emb_freeze', False) and self.dynamics._dt_embedding is not None:\\n            self.dynamics._dt_embedding.weight.requires_grad_(False)\\n        self.heads = nn.ModuleDict()\\n        if config.dyn_discrete:\\n            feat_size = config.dyn_stoch * config.dyn_discrete + config.dyn_deter\\n        else:\\n            feat_size = config.dyn_stoch + config.dyn_deter\\n        self.heads[\\\"decoder\\\"] = net\\n\\n... self._config.gate_fixed_dt > 0 else 1.0\\n                bsz = dts_float.shape[1]\\n                tr_h = torch.cat([\\n                    torch.full((1, bsz), init_dt_f, device=dts.device),\\n ```text
````
```text
Now I have a clear picture. Let me also look at the WorldModel._train() hook-based SGS code more carefully, and the Optimizer class to understand how gradients are handled.

Actually I already have the relevant code. Let me now analyze:

**Current full_sgs implementation has two parts:**

### Part 1: WorldModel._train() (lines ~346-358)
Hook-based: computes the gradient of all head losses (decoder+reward+cont) w.r.t. dt_emb.weight, then registers a backward hook that subtracts that gradient from the total gradient. This leaves only the dynamics (KL) gradient on dt_emb.weight.


### Part 2: ImagBehavior._train() (lines 642-644)
The issue is that dt_emb.weight.requires_grad gets disabled before actor/value optimization and never gets re-enabled. This causes a critical problem: when WorldModel._train() runs next, the gradient computation fails because torch.autograd.grad() returns None for tensors with requires_grad=False, effectively freezing dt_emb permanently. The fix is straightforward—restore requires_grad to True after the optimization block completes.

Now I'm reconsidering the actual training sequence. In DreamerV3, WorldModel trains first, then ImagBehavior trains second within each iteration. So after ImagBehavior disables dt_emb.requires_grad, the next iteration's WorldModel._train() call will encounter a frozen embedding layer. Let me verify whether WorldModel has any code that re-enables gradients for this parameter.

Looking at the conditional logic around line 230, it only freezes dt_emb when dt_emb_freeze is set, and there's no corresponding code to restore requires_grad_(True) at the start of training. This means once ImagBehavior freezes it, it stays frozen.

The real question is whether ImagBehavior even needs to freeze dt_emb in the first place. Since the actor loss already detaches the input features, the embedding shouldn't receive gradients anyway. So the value loss does flow gradients back through `imag_feat` to the dynamics module and `dt_emb`, even though the target is detached. The actor loss in "dynamics" mode has the same issue. That's why the `requires_grad_(False)` on `dt_emb` in ImagBehavior is necessary—to block these unintended gradient flows during the policy computation phase.

The solution is to disable gradients on the embedding during actor and value optimization, then restore them afterward so the world model can train normally in the next iteration.

Now I need to apply this fix to the remote server by editing models.py and running a dry-run to verify it works.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
Now I see the full picture. The ImagBehavior._train method has two separate `RequiresGrad` blocks:

1. `with tools.RequiresGrad(self.actor):` — for the actor loss computation (imagination + actor loss)
2. `with tools.RequiresGrad(self.value):` — for the value loss computation

And then at the end:
```python
with tools.RequiresGrad(self):
    if getattr(self._config, 'full_sgs', False) and self._world_model.dynamics._dt_embedding is not None:


```

Wait, I see now that actor loss computation is done inside `RequiresGrad(self.actor)` which only sets actor params to requires_grad. The value loss is computed inside `RequiresGrad(self.value)` which only sets value params. And importantly, the value input is detached before being passed to the value network, so the value computation doesn't flow gradients back through the world model. Then the optimizer steps are applied to each component separately.

For the actor loss, the input features are also detached before being fed to the actor network. But here's the key thing I'm realizing — the actor loss depends on target values computed from reward predictions and value estimates, which come from the world model's imagined trajectories. So even though the actor input is detached, the target computation itself is still connected to the world model's forward pass through the reward and value functions.

```

python
# RequiresGrad context pattern...
```

Examining the RequiresGrad pattern...

So the actor and value networks get their gradients enabled separately during their loss computations, but then during optimization, the entire ImagBehavior module has requires_grad enabled — and if full_sgs is true, dt_emb gets explicitly disabled to prevent gradient flow to it.

Now I'm checking what happens in the world model's training to see if its parameters are already set to requires_grad=False before the optimization step...
````

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
Now I understand the full picture. The key insight from the Optimizer class:

```python
def __call__(self, loss, params, retain_graph=True):
    self._opt.zero_grad()  # zeros grads for THIS optimizer's params only
    self._scaler.scale(loss).backward(retain_graph=retain_graph)
    ...
    self._opt.step()
    self._opt.zero_grad()  # zeros again after step
```

The optimizer calls `self._opt.zero_grad()` at the START and END. `self._opt` is created with specific parameters (e.g., actor params), so `zero_grad()` only zeros THOSE params' gradients. 


But `loss.backward()` computes gradients for ALL tensors in the graph with requires_grad=True. So when the actor optimizer runs, it zeros only actor gradients, but the backward pass computes gradients for both actor params and dt_emb.weight if they're connected in the computation graph. Then the step only updates actor params, and finally it zeros actor gradients again—leaving dt_emb.weight's gradients untouched for the next optimizer to use.

The value optimizer does the same thing, potentially accumulating more gradients into dt_emb.weight. But when the model optimizer runs next, it zeros ALL world model parameters' gradients including dt_emb, so those stale gradients from actor and value get properly cleared before the model update. The `requires_grad_(False)` is really just an optimization to skip computing unnecessary gradients in the first place.

Now I'm realizing there's a subtlety with `retain_graph=True` that affects how the computation graph persists after each backward pass. The issue is that `torch.autograd.grad` requires the target tensor to have `requires_grad=True`, so calling it on a frozen embedding will fail or return None. Then when the model optimizer runs its backward pass, the embedding gets no gradients at all since `requires_grad` is False, effectively freezing it permanently. This confirms the bug—the embedding never receives updates after the imagination step. The fix involves restoring `requires_grad=True` after the imagination phase completes.

I'm weighing three approaches: explicitly re-enabling gradients after the context block, using try/finally to ensure restoration, or removing the `requires_grad_(False)` entirely since the hook-based gradient control in WorldModel already prevents unwanted updates. The third option is cleanest for correctness, though it trades away a minor computation optimization.

But I'm realizing the hook-based approach only covers world model losses—the actor and value losses run their own backward passes that could accumulate gradients on dt_emb.weight. These gradients get zeroed at the next model_opt call, so they don't corrupt the model update, but with `retain_graph=True` in the optimizers, I need to verify whether gradients from the current iteration's actor and value losses are actually interfering with anything. The GradScaler just scales gradients without accumulating them across iterations, and zero_grad() properly clears everything, so my conclusion is to restore requires_grad_(True) after the context manager block—this keeps the performance optimization while being the minimal fix. I don't need to change the dt_emb_freeze logic in WorldModel._train() since that's intentionally permanent. Now I'm writing the patch to restore the gradient requirement after the full-SGS block. Completing the optimizer updates and then re-enabling gradients on the dt_embedding weight after the loss computation, unless the config specifies to keep it frozen. I'm considering whether to use a Python script instead of sed for more robust patching.
````

## 调研结果

**dt_emb 在各模块中的使用方式：**

1. **RSSM dynamics**: dt_emb 通过 GRU 融入 deter state → 保留梯度 ✓
2. **WorldModel._train() hooks (line 346-372)**: full_sgs 时用 gradient hook 减去所有 head loss (decoder+reward+cont) 对 dt_emb 的梯度 → 已实现 ✓
3. **ImagBehavior (line 642-644)**: full_sgs 时设 `dt_emb.weight.requires_grad_(False)` → **有 bug**

Dry-run 完成。下面是完整报告。

---

## 报告

### 1. dt_emb 在各 head 中的使用方式

| 模块 | dt_emb 参与方式 | SGS 策略 |
|------|----------------|----------|
| RSSM dynamics | dt_emb 融入 GRU deter state | **保留梯度**（唯一的 dt_emb 训练信号） |
| WorldModel heads (decoder/reward/cont) | 通过 feat=stoch+deter 间接包含 dt_emb | Hook 减去 head loss 梯度（line 346-358） |
| Actor | `_compute_actor_loss` 中 target 通过 imag_feat 连接 dt_emb | `requires_grad_(False)` 阻断（line 644） |
| Value | `value(value_input.detach())` — **已 detach** | 不需要额外处理（但 actor_opt backward 时 target 路径仍有连接） |

### 2. Full-SGS 实现方案和代码修改

**发现**: `full_sgs` flag 和核心逻辑已实现（configs.yaml line 121, models.py lines 346-358 + 642-644），但有一个 **关键 bug**。

**Bug**: `ImagBehavior._train()` line 644 设置 `dt_emb.weight.requires_grad_(False)` 后永不恢复。下个 iteration 的 `WorldModel._train()` 中 `torch.autograd.grad()` 返回 None → dynamics KL loss 也无法训练 dt_emb → **等同于 dt_emb_freeze**。

**修复** (1行代码): 在 `with RequiresGrad(self)` 块之后恢复 `requires_grad_(True)`:

```python
# models.py line 647-649 (新增)
if getattr(self._config, 'full_sgs', False) and self._world_model.dynamics._dt_embedding is not None:
    if not getattr(self._config, 'dt_emb_freeze', False):
        self._world_model.dynamics._dt_embedding.weight.requires_grad_(True)
```

### 3. Dry-run 结果

**成功** — 100 training updates，无报错。

关键 metrics:
- `model_loss=20.6`, `model_grad_norm=164.0` — 正常
- `actor_loss=-0.1`, `actor_grad_norm=0.0` — 正常（early training）
- `value_loss=9.2`, `value_grad_norm=17.8` — 正常
- `gate_dt_mean=2.0`, `gate_dt_2_frac=1.0` — 符合 `gate_fixed_dt=2`

### 4. 建议的提交命令

```bash
cd /root/autodl-tmp/event-gated-adaptive-horizon
source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base

LD_PRELOAD=/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu/libstdc++.so.6 MUJOCO_GL=egl nohup python -u dreamer.py \
  --configs defaults dmc_proprio \
  --task dmc_walker_walk \
  --logdir logdir/exp_018_full_sgs_walker_s1 \
  --steps 502000 --seed 1 --compile False --device cuda:1 \
  --gate_enabled True --gate_fixed_dt 2 --gate_subsample_max_dt 2 \
  --imag_horizon 8 --full_sgs True \
  > /tmp/exp_018_full_sgs.log 2>&1 &
```

注意：需等 cuda:1 上 010a（Cheetah）完成后再提交，否则可能 OOM。或确认 010a 显存用量后判断能否并行。
